---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes ??Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** ??does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** ??does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you ??run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point ??a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here ??not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) ??fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report ??per file/hunk where relevant ??every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** ??include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings ??the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes ??that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing ??**Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions ??**Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

# Round 32 of the relation-map review

Previous rounds: `audit_evidence_claims/m1` to `m31` (`REPORT.md`; m27 and m29 stopped early, so their `attacks/` folders are the record). All repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`. Review the latest commit on HEAD. Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 361. Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>` on a port other than 8765 and exercise it over HTTP.

## What changed since round 31: a design change, not another word-list repair

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's product markers did not cover. The relation map no longer depends on those markers. See the section "Before round 32" in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, and `src/app.py` `Engine.explore`, `_sig`, `_latin`.

- **P32-1.** `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name. The linker must read exactly one entity, and its matched text must have the same letters, digits and symbols as the input (`_sig`: NFKC, casefold, any script). Case, spaces, punctuation and ®/™/© do not count. Anything else returns `not_one_name`, and the page asks for the name alone.
- **P32-2.** If any part of the input is refused, the whole input is refused.
- **P32-3.** A name stored for several live nodes is not drawn. The reply is `choose`: the nodes are listed and the reader clicks one, which loads it by id.
- **P32-4.** Lower-case `nct`/`chembl`/`ensg` ids are read. The whole input can be a company key or target name when `_latin(text)` equals the dictionary key, so a symbol the key would drop still blocks it (for example "Merck & Co").
- **P32-5.** This change is in the question linker, `src/query.py` `_whole_name` and the end of `_link`. A canonical key shared by several drugs is no longer decided by its spelling; only a drug's own preferred name chooses it. Inside such a key, a shorter name is not read either. Before, "medi4736" read as atezolizumab and "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine.
- **New tests:** `tests/test_app.py::test_the_map_draws_only_a_whole_stored_name` and `test_a_key_shared_by_several_drugs_is_not_decided_by_spelling`. Older map tests that drew a centre from a longer input now go through `_page`: the map must not draw, and the question linker's reading is checked instead.
- **Docs:** README defect 15 (both languages) records that the question boxes still rely on the linker's markers for free text. Defect 9 records the shared keys. Section 0 of `web/try.html` states the one-name rule.

## Scope and approval bar

The subject is the relation map: section 0 of `web/try.html`, `POST /api/explore`, `Engine.explore`. The guarantees are those in the original brief below, with guarantee 3 now read as follows: the map draws a node only for an input that is, as a whole, one stored name of that node (or its id). It never draws a refused product, tracer or conjugate as the originator, and never draws one of several nodes that share a name.

1. Re-run every earlier reproduction from m1 to m31 against the map, and mark each finding FIXED or NOT FIXED. Reuse the earlier scripts. Inputs that were longer than a name should now be refused, `not_one_name`, `choose` or `not_found`, never a drawn originator.
2. Attack the new rule:
   - any input that draws a node the input does not name as a whole
   - a whole stored name that is in fact a different product (a data problem; say so, and separate it from code problems)
   - Unicode, symbols and invisible characters that `_sig`/`_latin` mishandle
   - the id path
   - the `choose` reply (live nodes only, injection-safe page markup, click-through)
   - company/target whole-name reads
   - retired nodes
   - anything the page text claims that the code does not do
3. Check P32-5 for regressions in the question boxes (2 to 4) and the demo examples. Report over-refusals as should-fix, not blockers.
4. Re-check guarantees A to G of the original brief briefly; earlier rounds approved them.

### Out of scope as blockers (recorded)

- The question boxes' free-text refusal relies on word lists (README defect 15). A leak there is a note, not a map blocker.
- Known data limitations, awaiting the owner's curation decision:
  - ChEMBL lists bevacizumab biosimilar development codes as bevacizumab synonyms.
  - An AACT synonym maps 13-cis-retinoic acid to CHEMBL38 tretinoin (M23-D1).
  - "Roche" is stored as a folic acid alias. The map now asks the reader to choose.
- Known over-refusals listed in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` (M5-S2, rapamycin forms, Trastuzumab--vcmmae conjugate, 9-amino-20(s)-camptothecin, il-6ra, TAK-659F, GS-11CAL-101, 3en3hg4wsw, an ADC named with its own payload beside it). A longer input not being drawn is the new rule, not an over-refusal.

Approval bar: no known way for the map to draw a node for an input that is not, as a whole, one stored name or id of it. That includes a refused product drawn as the originator, one of several same-named nodes drawn without the reader's choice, a wrong relation or direction, a value that differs from the stored one, and script injection. Report should-fix items separately from blockers.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m32/`. Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m32/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Round 34 of the relation-map review

## Before you start

- Read the round-32 brief, `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`, for the scope, guarantees and approval bar. Read the round-33 report at `audit_evidence_claims/m33/REPORT.md`.
- Repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, section "Round 33".
- Review the latest commit on HEAD.
- Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 362.
- Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>`, on any port except 8765.

## What changed since round 33

The changes are in `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__` and `Engine.explore`, and in `src/query.py`.

- **Drugs are read only by their ChEMBL preferred name.** The map still compares names by `_sig`, as in round 33.
  - Every other stored name of a drug goes into `alias_only`: trade names, ChEMBL synonyms and registry (AACT) synonyms. Typing one returns the reply `alias_only`, and the page asks for the preferred name.
  - This replaces round 33's partial source exclusions, which had the replies `registry_name` and `biologic_synonym`.
  - Why: the round-33 data findings showed that trade names, like synonyms, file combination products, formulations and biosimilars under one molecule.
- **The map also reads:** every node's own label, target symbols, and company and disease keys. The company and disease keys only match input whose `_sig` equals the dictionary key.
- **A refused stored name stays refused, however it is spelt.** The question linker checks every stored name that matches the input's `_sig`, whether that name is read or `alias_only`. If any is refused, the input is refused. This repairs M33-B1: `Myocet™`, `My/o/cet`, `Inf/lectra` and `Megace/ES` are now refused.
- **More names in `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` (`src/query.py`).** The question boxes now also refuse the round-33 combination products, plus PRED-G, NEO-DELTA-CORTEF and LEP-ETU.
- **The preferred-name rule for a shared key in a question** now needs the name to be written as a word of its own. This repairs M33-S1, "(+/-)NICOTINE".
- **Docs:** README defects 9 and 15 in both languages, and section 0 of `web/try.html`.

## What to do

1. Re-run every round-33 finding (M33-B1, D1–D6, S1, S2) and the round-32 findings. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED, with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays against the map.
2. Attack the design. Look for:
   - any input drawn for a node of which it is not, as a whole, one read name under `_sig`;
   - a read name (a ChEMBL preferred name, a node label, a target symbol, a company or disease key) that denotes a different product from the node drawn;
   - a stored name the question linker refuses that the map still draws under some spelling;
   - Unicode problems, the `choose` and `alias_only` replies and their markup, retired nodes, and the id path.
3. Check the question-linker changes for regressions in the question boxes and the demos.
4. Briefly re-check guarantees A–G.

Report data problems separately from code problems. For each data problem give the exact input, the source record and an independent identity reference.

## Not blockers (recorded)

- Free-text leaks in the question boxes (README defect 15).
- The resolver's own brand lists in graph construction (README defect 15).
- Known over-refusals:
  - the list in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`;
  - brands and development codes that are only synonyms, which the map does not draw;
  - two tracer drugs, refused by their own labels.

## Output

- Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m34/`.
- Put the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m34/REPORT.md`.
- Do not edit tracked files.
- End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

# Round 37 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief (`audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`) for scope, guarantees and the approval bar, the round-34 brief for the name policy (`audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md`), and the round-36 report (`audit_evidence_claims/m36/REPORT.md`). Repairs: `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` section "Round 36". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 365). Page: `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 36

- `src/app.py` `_sig` no longer interprets optical-rotation descriptors. After trademark signs, compatibility folding, case, marks (accents) and invisible characters are removed, a name that contains a bracketed group (any Unicode opening/closing or initial/final quotation punctuation) holding no letter or digit is compared character for character: only spaces are dropped and every dash (category Pd, U+2212) is read as "-". Every other name keeps the round-32 rule (spaces and punctuation dropped). Stored names go through the same function, so a descriptor name matches only its own spelling (spaces, case, accents, invisible characters and dash style aside).
- `src/query.py` `_named_alone`: any sign (+, -, minus, plus-minus) in the run of brackets, signs, dashes, slashes, underscores and spaces next to a shared-key name in a question blocks the preferred-name reading.

## What to do

1. Re-run M36-B1, B2, S1 and every round-32 to round-35 finding; FIXED / NOT FIXED with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays and the expanded source universe.
2. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief, including the two comparison modes and which mode a name falls into.
3. Check the question-linker change for regressions in the question boxes and demos.
4. Re-check guarantees A–G briefly.

Report data problems separately from code problems. Recorded, not blockers: question-box free-text leaks and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15); the over-refusals in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, synonyms and brands the map does not draw, descriptor spellings other than the stored one, two tracer drugs and preferred names written like a refused spelling; THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m37/`, the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m37/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.
FILE: audit_evidence_claims/m37/REPORT.md

The exact M36-B1, M36-B2 and M36-S1 reproductions are FIXED.** Two question-only changes and incomplete user-facing documentation are recorded separately below. FIXED closes the exact reproduction under the current policy; it does not mean alias recall or upstream source curation has been restored. This is an independent review, with no production repair.

The full run has no warnings. [Output](pytest.txt), [command and timing](pytest_meta.json).
- Started the actual CLI **`python src/app.py 8937`**. Root retained **327 HTTP response records**: 124 baseline, 85 exact/supplementary cases, 36 question/data follow-ups, 70 round-37 repair/mode cases, seven underscore confirmations and five documentation controls. These are retained records, not a count of all requests in every lane. The expected empty-input and 501-character baseline requests return HTTP 400. The served page equals `web/try.html` byte for byte. There are zero exceptions, independent admitted-name membership failures, silent ambiguous draws or wrong choice sets. ## Round-36 repairs

Parent comparisons load **both parent `app.py` and parent `query.py`**, and current outcomes are separately confirmed through the CLI HTTP server. Unicode escapes below denote actual characters. [Reproducer](root_round37.py), [parent/current/HTTP evidence](root_round37_http.json), [compact results](root_round37_summary.json).

| Finding | Disposition | Exact inputs and current result |
|---|---|---|
| M36-B1 | **FIXED** | `(-_)-ARTEMISININ`, `(_-)-ARTEMISININ`, `[-_]-ARTEMISININ`, `"(-\u200b_\u0301)-ARTEMISININ"`, `"(-\uff3f)-ARTEMISININ"`, `"(-\ufe4d)-ARTEMISININ"`, `(-())-ARTEMISININ`, `〔-〕-ARTEMISININ`, `«-»-ARTEMISININ` all return `not_one_name`; parent draws CHEMBL269671. Each of the seven underscore characters U+005F, U+FE33, U+FE34, U+FE4D, U+FE4E, U+FE4F and U+FF3F inside `(-<character>)-ARTEMISININ` is separately confirmed undrawn over HTTP. |
| M36-B2 | **FIXED** | `(+/-☢)NICOTINE`, `(±☢)NICOTINE`, `(+/-$)NICOTINE`, `(+/-×)NICOTINE` return `not_one_name`; `(+/☢-)NICOTINE`, `(+/×-)NICOTINE` return `not_found`. All six previously drew CHEMBL440464. Significant symbols remain in the signature. |
| M36-S1 | **FIXED exact questions** | `What trials evaluate ((+/-))NICOTINE?` and `What does NICOTINE((+/-)) target?`: no linked entity and zero evidence in `/api/ask`, `/api/structured`, and `/api/check`; map `not_found`. Parent reads CHEMBL3 and retrieves two rows. |

Positive controls remain valid: `ARTEMISININ` and `CHEMBL269671` draw CHEMBL269671; `(+/-)NICOTINE`, `( + / - )NICOTINE`, `(+/–)NICOTINE`, `(+/-)nicotine`, fullwidth `（＋/－）NICOTINE`, and accented `(+/-)NÍCOTINE` draw CHEMBL440464. `(±)-nicotine`, `(+-)NICOTINE`, `(-+)-NICOTINE` and `()osimertinib` remain undrawn under the revised policy. [Controls](root_round37_summary.json).

## Independent attack on the two comparison modes

The oracle reconstructs names and candidate IDs from the dictionary and SQL records; it does not use `Engine.names`. Its normalization classifier is a separate character scanner rather than the implementation's `_GROUP` regular expression. [Oracle and source sweep](source/source_audit.py), [composition probes](source/modes.py), [boundary probes](source/mode_boundaries.py).

The critical separation is structural: a strict-mode signature retains at least one opening/initial-quotation and one closing/final-quotation punctuation character. An ordinary-mode signature removes all punctuation. Those signature spaces cannot collide. This argument depends on the promised preprocessing order: trademark removal, compatibility/case folding and mark/invisible removal precede classification; space removal and dash normalization cannot erase those retained delimiters. It supports mode separation, not universal chemical equivalence of ordinary punctuation-normalized names.

- **22,956 source/key inputs × 14 transforms = 321,384 comparisons.** Fourteen literal source spellings use strict mode; 22,942 use ordinary mode. No implementation-versus-independent-oracle mismatch was found. There are 42 mode-dependent punctuation-transform differences, which are expected under the new policy. Two additional generic `swapcase()` invariance probes differ for Turkish dotless `ı`; both agree with the prescribed casefold oracle. Those probe assumptions are not silently counted as passing or as new map defects. [Summary](source/source_mode_summary.json), [differences](source/source_mode_differences.json), [follow-up outcomes](source/mode_followup.json).
- **11,086 assigned punctuation/mark/symbol/space characters × four contexts = 44,344 cases**, plus all **91 opening × 87 closing delimiters = 7,917 pairs**: zero oracle failures or unsafe plain-ARTEMISININ collisions. [Unicode summary](source/unicode_mode_summary.json).
- **138,557 assigned Unicode letter/number characters** inserted into a candidate group: zero classifier disagreements. Sixteen lose their letter/number category during the specified compatibility/mark preprocessing and correctly change mode afterwards. [Classification results](source/letter_number_classification.json).
- Nested and mismatched brackets, Unicode quotations, empty groups, fullwidth forms, signs, unrelated symbols, letters/digits inside a group and preprocessing-created empty groups were exercised. **28 HTTP boundary controls** supplement root's confirmations. `(-ARTEMISININ)` and `(-A)RTEMISININ` have letters inside their only group and therefore use ordinary mode, drawing ARTEMISININ; ASCII apostrophes are category Po, not the designated Pi/Pf quotation delimiters. These results follow the explicit new classifier and inherited ordinary punctuation rule. They are disclosed design boundaries, not evidence that the code interprets arbitrary strings as optical descriptors. [Boundary evidence](source/mode_boundary_http.json).
- **182 literal refused spellings × 13 variants = 2,366, all refused.** A separate 84 synthetic canonical-only keys × 13 = 1,092 cases yield 1,074 refusals, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`, no centers. All **1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396** remain undrawn. **54 private retirement cases / ten assertion groups** pass. [Classified refusal results](source/refusal_variants_classified.json), [raw summary](source/refusal_variants_summary.json), [suffixes](source/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement fixture](source/retired_private_fixture.json).

## New nonblocking findings

### M37-S1 — punctuation or a symbol can end the question descriptor scan before its sign

**Question-linker regression**, `src/query.py:118` and `src/query.py:133–135`. The parent accepted arbitrary nonword characters inside a sign group. The new adjacent run excludes punctuation such as `.` and symbols such as `☢` and `$`. Looking left from NICOTINE, the scan stops on that character before reaching the sign.

| Exact input | Parent question result | HEAD question result | HEAD map |
|---|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate (+/-.)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-☢)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-$)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE(.+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |

The same new reading occurs with `×`, `:` and `,`, including mirrored suffix forms. The combined **28-question / 112-HTTP** probe has **12 newly admitted question readings** and no map centers. `/api/ask` and `/api/check` expose the entity; `/api/structured` retrieves the same two evidence rows. The opposite-side punctuation controls remain blocked because the scan meets a sign first. This is a loss of question refusal coverage, not a map admission failure; it is nonblocking under README defect 15 and the explicit review scope. [Prefix/suffix evidence](contract/question_punctuation_regression.json), [mirrored suffix evidence](contract/question_punctuation_mirror.json).

### M37-S2 — ordinary sign-separated lists lose a shared preferred name

**Question recall/design cost**, `src/query.py:133–135`. `What trials evaluate NICOTINE + osimertinib?` previously linked CHEMBL3 and CHEMBL3353410; now only CHEMBL3353410 remains. The selected evidence consequently changes from two NICOTINE rows to 12 osimertinib rows. `What trials evaluate osimertinib + NICOTINE?` and `What trials evaluate NICOTINE - osimertinib?` also drop NICOTINE. The `/` and `and` controls retain both entities.

This follows the requested rule that **any adjacent sign** blocks a shared-key preferred-name reading. It should be understood as a recall tradeoff, rather than claimed to violate that new rule. All such complete questions remain undrawn by the map. The expanded supplement tests **six names × 30 patterns × two wrappers = 360 cases**, with 74 parent-query changes and zero map centers; not all 74 changes are defects. [Supplement and parent comparison](contract/question_supplement.json), [summary](contract/question_supplement_summary.json), [embedded HTTP records](contract/question_supplement.json).

Previously existing free-text leaks are separate: `What trials evaluate 〔+/-〕NICOTINE?`, `What trials evaluate NICOTINE«+/-»?` and `"What trials evaluate (+/-)\u200bNICOTINE?"` still read CHEMBL3 with two evidence rows, identically to the parent. These remain recorded question-box limitations. Ordinary `(NICOTINE)` recall and the exact M36 nested-descriptor repair pass.

### M37-S3 — page and explore documentation omit the new punctuation exception

**Documentation should-fix**, `web/try.html:54` and `src/app.py:110–115`. Their unqualified claim that punctuation is ignored no longer describes every name. The new `_sig` docstring explains the exception, but the visible page does not. For example, `(+/-)NICOTINE` draws while `(+/-)NICO/TINE`, `(+/-)NICOTINE!`, or bracket changes do not; `()osimertinib` is also undrawn. Explain that a bracketed group without letters/digits switches the whole name to literal-punctuation comparison. These are accepted recall exclusions, so the wording issue does not negate the map safety result. [Exact HTTP controls](documentation_http.json).

## Expanded source universe

The fresh sweep covers **22,956 distinct source/key inputs from 34,429 records**, including **all 9,581 `dictionary.drugs[*].aliases` entries**, drug preferred names, index spellings and keys, SQL node labels, target symbols, and company/disease keys. It includes **16,015 literal source spellings/read keys and 6,941 additional canonical-only index strings**, against 6,469 live nodes.

Results: **10,088 centers, 12,486 `alias_only`, 354 refused, eight choices, five `not_found`, 15 `not_one_name`**. Independent membership, stored-refusal, silent-ambiguity and live-choice assertions have **zero failures and zero exceptions**. The sweep completed in **947.748 seconds**. All 1,696 dictionary alias occurrences omitted from the lookup index were included in the source inventory. [Source inventory and pointers](source/all_source_records.json), [responses](source/source_sweep.json), [assertions/counts](source/source_summary.json), [source classes](source/source_classified_summary.json), [index omissions](source/source_discrepancies.json).

There are **zero response changes against the saved M36 source results**. This is fresh HEAD execution compared with saved prior responses, not a newly rerun parent Engine sweep. The historical corpus comparison uses the same saved-prior-results distinction; the focused repair and question regression comparisons separately execute parent code. The complete source inventory and the independently specified mode oracle are both needed: matching the implementation's own signatures alone would not have detected the normalization faults in previous rounds. [Comparison](source/source_parent_changes.json), [source findings and limitations](source/findings.md).

## Round 4 (audit_evidence_claims/m4) — CHANGES REQUIRED

Shortest-span binding kept failing (wrong drug on ties, a six-token reach, qualifiers already consumed by the token pass).
Replaced by a fail-closed rule: text is cut into list items at , ; ， ； 、 and listing words; an item in which any run of
words names a distinct product or tracer has every drug in it refused (which drug the qualifier describes is not guessed);
drugs in other items, and targets/companies in the same item, still link. M4-B1 (both drugs now refused, never
BEVACIZUMAB), M4-B2 (no distance cap), M4-B3 (one pass, no pre-consumed qualifier) and the EGFR/AstraZeneca over-refusal
are covered by tests. Tests 326.

A negation covers only the label word it negates ("non-labelled", "non-radio-labelled"), not a later label in the same item. Stored-alias sweep: no change. Tests 350.

## Before round 32 — the map takes one whole name (design change, not a word-list repair)

Rounds 20 to 31 each found new wording that the question linker's markers did not cover
(a phrase between the joining word and the partner, dye names outside the list). A list of
words cannot be complete, so the map no longer depends on it:

| # | Change | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P32-1 | `explore` draws only when the whole input is one name: the linker reads exactly one entity and its matched text has the same letters, digits and symbols (any script; case, spaces, punctuation, trademark signs aside) as the input. Anything more returns `not_one_name` and the page asks for the name alone | a word the linker does not know can make the name a different product; now an unknown word means "not drawn", whatever it is |
| P32-2 | any refused part refuses the whole input (before, "osimertinib, Mvasi" drew osimertinib and listed Mvasi) | one input, one name |
| P32-3 | a name stored for several nodes is not drawn; the nodes are listed and the reader picks one by id ("Roche": folic acid alias and the company) | before, the first was drawn and the others listed |
| P32-4 | lower-case ids (nct..., chembl..., ensg...) and company names the linker cuts at "&" ("Merck & Co") are read whole | recall for one-name input |
| P32-5 | the question linker no longer decides a key shared by several drugs by its spelling, and does not fall back to a shorter name inside such a key | found by a sweep of the shared keys the earlier sweeps skipped: "medi4736" was read as atezolizumab, "5-fluorouracil" as capecitabine, "ak112" as envafolimab, "peginterferon alfa-2b" as alfa-2a, "crlx101" as camptothecin, "PD-1 mAb" as pembrolizumab, "Dha paclitaxel" as paclitaxel |

The question boxes still use the linker's markers on free text; that limit is now README
defect 15. Corpus check of the question linker (9,746 intervention strings and demo
questions): 20 change, all names whose key is shared by several drugs, now unread instead of
read as one of them. Tests 361.

Known recall limits (fail closed): registry-only names ("IL-2", "9-ac", "5-fluorouracil", "interferon α-2b") are not drawn; two tracer drugs are refused by their own labels. Corpus check of the question linker: Equidacent and INFLECTRA® are now refused; nothing else changed. Antibody development codes (MK-3475, MEDI4736) are not drawn by the map. Tests 362.

 _SIGNS = re.compile(r"[+\-−±]")
 
 
 def _named_alone(question: str, name: str) -> bool:
     """The name occurs as a word of its own with no optical-rotation descriptor beside
     it: "NICOTINE" and "(NICOTINE)", but not "(+/-)NICOTINE", "(+/-)(NICOTINE)",
-    "((+/-))NICOTINE", "NICOTINE (+)" or a name glued to other letters or signs. Any
-    sign in the run of brackets, signs and spaces next to the name counts."""
+    "((+/-))NICOTINE", "(+/-.)NICOTINE", "NICOTINE (+)" or a name glued to other letters
+    or signs. Any sign in the run of non-letters next to the name counts, so a list
+    joined by "+" or "-" ("NICOTINE + osimertinib") does not read it either."""
     for m in re.finditer(re.escape(name), question, re.I):
         before, after = question[:m.start()], question[m.end():]

**APPROVE within the specified relation-map scope. The exact M36-B1, M36-B2 and M36-S1 reproductions are FIXED.** Two question-only changes and incomplete user-facing documentation are recorded separately below. FIXED closes the exact reproduction under the current policy; it does not mean alias recall or upstream source curation has been restored. This is an independent review, with no production repair.

## Independent attack on the two comparison modes

The oracle reconstructs names and candidate IDs from the dictionary and SQL records; it does not use `Engine.names`. Its normalization classifier is a separate character scanner rather than the implementation's `_GROUP` regular expression. [Oracle and source sweep](source/source_audit.py), [composition probes](source/modes.py), [boundary probes](source/mode_boundaries.py).

The critical separation is structural: a strict-mode signature retains at least one opening/initial-quotation and one closing/final-quotation punctuation character. An ordinary-mode signature removes all punctuation. Those signature spaces cannot collide. This argument depends on the promised preprocessing order: trademark removal, compatibility/case folding and mark/invisible removal precede classification; space removal and dash normalization cannot erase those retained delimiters. It supports mode separation, not universal chemical equivalence of ordinary punctuation-normalized names.

- **22,956 source/key inputs × 14 transforms = 321,384 comparisons.** Fourteen literal source spellings use strict mode; 22,942 use ordinary mode. No implementation-versus-independent-oracle mismatch was found. There are 42 mode-dependent punctuation-transform differences, which are expected under the new policy. Two additional generic `swapcase()` invariance probes differ for Turkish dotless `ı`; both agree with the prescribed casefold oracle. Those probe assumptions are not silently counted as passing or as new map defects. [Summary](source/source_mode_summary.json), [differences](source/source_mode_differences.json), [follow-up outcomes](source/mode_followup.json).
- **11,086 assigned punctuation/mark/symbol/space characters × four contexts = 44,344 cases**, plus all **91 opening × 87 closing delimiters = 7,917 pairs**: zero oracle failures or unsafe plain-ARTEMISININ collisions. [Unicode summary](source/unicode_mode_summary.json).
- **138,557 assigned Unicode letter/number characters** inserted into a candidate group: zero classifier disagreements. Sixteen lose their letter/number category during the specified compatibility/mark preprocessing and correctly change mode afterwards. [Classification results](source/letter_number_classification.json).
- Nested and mismatched brackets, Unicode quotations, empty groups, fullwidth forms, signs, unrelated symbols, letters/digits inside a group and preprocessing-created empty groups were exercised. **28 HTTP boundary controls** supplement root's confirmations. `(-ARTEMISININ)` and `(-A)RTEMISININ` have letters inside their only group and therefore use ordinary mode, drawing ARTEMISININ; ASCII apostrophes are category Po, not the designated Pi/Pf quotation delimiters. These results follow the explicit new classifier and inherited ordinary punctuation rule. They are disclosed design boundaries, not evidence that the code interprets arbitrary strings as optical descriptors. [Boundary evidence](source/mode_boundary_http.json).
- **182 literal refused spellings × 13 variants = 2,366, all refused.** A separate 84 synthetic canonical-only keys × 13 = 1,092 cases yield 1,074 refusals, 12 `alias_only`, six `not_found`, no centers. All **1,066 preferred names × six significant suffixes = 6,396** remain undrawn. **54 private retirement cases / ten assertion groups** pass. [Classified refusal results](source/refusal_variants_classified.json), [raw summary](source/refusal_variants_summary.json), [suffixes](source/significant_suffixes_summary.json), [retirement fixture](source/retired_private_fixture.json).

## New nonblocking findings

### M37-S1 — punctuation or a symbol can end the question descriptor scan before its sign

**Question-linker regression**, `src/query.py:118` and `src/query.py:133–135`. The parent accepted arbitrary nonword characters inside a sign group. The new adjacent run excludes punctuation such as `.` and symbols such as `☢` and `$`. Looking left from NICOTINE, the scan stops on that character before reaching the sign.

| Exact input | Parent question result | HEAD question result | HEAD map |
|---|---|---|---|
| `What trials evaluate (+/-.)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-☢)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate (+/-$)NICOTINE?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |
| `What trials evaluate NICOTINE(.+/-)?` | no entity/evidence | CHEMBL3; two evidence rows | `not_one_name` |

The same new reading occurs with `×`, `:` and `,`, including mirrored suffix forms. The combined **28-question / 112-HTTP** probe has **12 newly admitted question readings** and no map centers. `/api/ask` and `/api/check` expose the entity; `/api/structured` retrieves the same two evidence rows. The opposite-side punctuation controls remain blocked because the scan meets a sign first. This is a loss of question refusal coverage, not a map admission failure; it is nonblocking under README defect 15 and the explicit review scope. [Prefix/suffix evidence](contract/question_punctuation_regression.json), [mirrored suffix evidence](contract/question_punctuation_mirror.json).

### M37-S2 — ordinary sign-separated lists lose a shared preferred name

**Question recall/design cost**, `src/query.py:133–135`. `What trials evaluate NICOTINE + osimertinib?` previously linked CHEMBL3 and CHEMBL3353410; now only CHEMBL3353410 remains. The selected evidence consequently changes from two NICOTINE rows to 12 osimertinib rows. `What trials evaluate osimertinib + NICOTINE?` and `What trials evaluate NICOTINE - osimertinib?` also drop NICOTINE. The `/` and `and` controls retain both entities.

This follows the requested rule that **any adjacent sign** blocks a shared-key preferred-name reading. It should be understood as a recall tradeoff, rather than claimed to violate that new rule. All such complete questions remain undrawn by the map. The expanded supplement tests **six names × 30 patterns × two wrappers = 360 cases**, with 74 parent-query changes and zero map centers; not all 74 changes are defects. [Supplement and parent comparison](contract/question_supplement.json), [summary](contract/question_supplement_summary.json), [embedded HTTP records](contract/question_supplement.json).

Previously existing free-text leaks are separate: `What trials evaluate 〔+/-〕NICOTINE?`, `What trials evaluate NICOTINE«+/-»?` and `"What trials evaluate (+/-)\u200bNICOTINE?"` still read CHEMBL3 with two evidence rows, identically to the parent. These remain recorded question-box limitations. Ordinary `(NICOTINE)` recall and the exact M36 nested-descriptor repair pass.

### M37-S3 — page and explore documentation omit the new punctuation exception

**Documentation should-fix**, `web/try.html:54` and `src/app.py:110–115`. Their unqualified claim that punctuation is ignored no longer describes every name. The new `_sig` docstring explains the exception, but the visible page does not. For example, `(+/-)NICOTINE` draws while `(+/-)NICO/TINE`, `(+/-)NICOTINE!`, or bracket changes do not; `()osimertinib` is also undrawn. Explain that a bracketed group without letters/digits switches the whole name to literal-punctuation comparison. These are accepted recall exclusions, so the wording issue does not negate the map safety result. [Exact HTTP controls](documentation_http.json).

No newly admitted preferred name, node label, symbol or key was independently proved to denote a different product from the drawn node. This is a bounded review result, not scientific certification of every source identity.

# Round 37 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief (`audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`) for scope, guarantees and the approval bar, the round-34 brief for the name policy (`audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md`), and the round-36 report (`audit_evidence_claims/m36/REPORT.md`). Repairs: `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` section "Round 36". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 365). Page: `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 36

- `src/app.py` `_sig` no longer interprets optical-rotation descriptors. After trademark signs, compatibility folding, case, marks (accents) and invisible characters are removed, a name that contains a bracketed group (any Unicode opening/closing or initial/final quotation punctuation) holding no letter or digit is compared character for character: only spaces are dropped and every dash (category Pd, U+2212) is read as "-". Every other name keeps the round-32 rule (spaces and punctuation dropped). Stored names go through the same function, so a descriptor name matches only its own spelling (spaces, case, accents, invisible characters and dash style aside).
- `src/query.py` `_named_alone`: any sign (+, -, minus, plus-minus) in the run of brackets, signs, dashes, slashes, underscores and spaces next to a shared-key name in a question blocks the preferred-name reading.

## What to do

1. Re-run M36-B1, B2, S1 and every round-32 to round-35 finding; FIXED / NOT FIXED with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays and the expanded source universe.
2. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief, including the two comparison modes and which mode a name falls into.
3. Check the question-linker change for regressions in the question boxes and demos.
4. Re-check guarantees A–G briefly.

Report data problems separately from code problems. Recorded, not blockers: question-box free-text leaks and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15); the over-refusals in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, synonyms and brands the map does not draw, descriptor spellings other than the stored one, two tracer drugs and preferred names written like a refused spelling; THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m37/`, the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m37/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.
# Round 34 of the relation-map review

## Before you start

- Read the round-32 brief, `audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`, for the scope, guarantees and approval bar. Read the round-33 report at `audit_evidence_claims/m33/REPORT.md`.
- Repairs are in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, section "Round 33".
- Review the latest commit on HEAD.
- Run the tests with `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider`; expect 362.
- Start the page with `python src/app.py <port>`, on any port except 8765.

## What changed since round 33

The changes are in `src/app.py` `Engine.__init__` and `Engine.explore`, and in `src/query.py`.

- **Drugs are read only by their ChEMBL preferred name.** The map still compares names by `_sig`, as in round 33.
  - Every other stored name of a drug goes into `alias_only`: trade names, ChEMBL synonyms and registry (AACT) synonyms. Typing one returns the reply `alias_only`, and the page asks for the preferred name.
  - This replaces round 33's partial source exclusions, which had the replies `registry_name` and `biologic_synonym`.
  - Why: the round-33 data findings showed that trade names, like synonyms, file combination products, formulations and biosimilars under one molecule.
- **The map also reads:** every node's own label, target symbols, and company and disease keys. The company and disease keys only match input whose `_sig` equals the dictionary key.
- **A refused stored name stays refused, however it is spelt.** The question linker checks every stored name that matches the input's `_sig`, whether that name is read or `alias_only`. If any is refused, the input is refused. This repairs M33-B1: `Myocet™`, `My/o/cet`, `Inf/lectra` and `Megace/ES` are now refused.
- **More names in `_OTHER_PRODUCT_NAMES` (`src/query.py`).** The question boxes now also refuse the round-33 combination products, plus PRED-G, NEO-DELTA-CORTEF and LEP-ETU.
- **The preferred-name rule for a shared key in a question** now needs the name to be written as a word of its own. This repairs M33-S1, "(+/-)NICOTINE".
- **Docs:** README defects 9 and 15 in both languages, and section 0 of `web/try.html`.

## What to do

1. Re-run every round-33 finding (M33-B1, D1–D6, S1, S2) and the round-32 findings. Mark each FIXED or NOT FIXED, with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays against the map.
2. Attack the design. Look for:
   - any input drawn for a node of which it is not, as a whole, one read name under `_sig`;
   - a read name (a ChEMBL preferred name, a node label, a target symbol, a company or disease key) that denotes a different product from the node drawn;
   - a stored name the question linker refuses that the map still draws under some spelling;
   - Unicode problems, the `choose` and `alias_only` replies and their markup, retired nodes, and the id path.
3. Check the question-linker changes for regressions in the question boxes and the demos.
4. Briefly re-check guarantees A–G.

Report data problems separately from code problems. For each data problem give the exact input, the source record and an independent identity reference.

## Not blockers (recorded)

- Free-text leaks in the question boxes (README defect 15).
- The resolver's own brand lists in graph construction (README defect 15).
- Known over-refusals:
  - the list in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`;
  - brands and development codes that are only synonyms, which the map does not draw;
  - two tracer drugs, refused by their own labels.

# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

# Round 37 of the relation-map review

Read the round-32 brief (`audit_evidence_claims/m32/brief.md`) for scope, guarantees and the approval bar, the round-34 brief for the name policy (`audit_evidence_claims/m34/brief.md`), and the round-36 report (`audit_evidence_claims/m36/REPORT.md`). Repairs: `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md` section "Round 36". Review the latest commit on HEAD. Tests: `python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (expect 365). Page: `python src/app.py <port>` (not 8765).

## What changed since round 36

- `src/app.py` `_sig` no longer interprets optical-rotation descriptors. After trademark signs, compatibility folding, case, marks (accents) and invisible characters are removed, a name that contains a bracketed group (any Unicode opening/closing or initial/final quotation punctuation) holding no letter or digit is compared character for character: only spaces are dropped and every dash (category Pd, U+2212) is read as "-". Every other name keeps the round-32 rule (spaces and punctuation dropped). Stored names go through the same function, so a descriptor name matches only its own spelling (spaces, case, accents, invisible characters and dash style aside).
- `src/query.py` `_named_alone`: any sign (+, -, minus, plus-minus) in the run of brackets, signs, dashes, slashes, underscores and spaces next to a shared-key name in a question blocks the preferred-name reading.

## What to do

1. Re-run M36-B1, B2, S1 and every round-32 to round-35 finding; FIXED / NOT FIXED with exact inputs. Re-run the historical replays and the expanded source universe.
2. Attack the design as in the round-34 brief, including the two comparison modes and which mode a name falls into.
3. Check the question-linker change for regressions in the question boxes and demos.
4. Re-check guarantees A–G briefly.

Report data problems separately from code problems. Recorded, not blockers: question-box free-text leaks and the resolver's own lists (README defect 15); the over-refusals in `CODEX_MAP_M1_VERDICT.md`, synonyms and brands the map does not draw, descriptor spellings other than the stored one, two tracer drugs and preferred names written like a refused spelling; THEOPHYLLINE's upstream drug type.

Write scratch files only under `audit_evidence_claims/m37/`, the report in `audit_evidence_claims/m37/REPORT.md`. Do not edit tracked files. End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.
import os, sys, json, subprocess, hashlib, time, pathlib, socket
ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
OUT = ROOT / 'audit_evidence_claims/m37'
def write(name, obj):
    (OUT/name).write_text(json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')
def hashes():
    files = subprocess.check_output(['git','ls-files','-z'], cwd=ROOT).decode().split('\0')
    files = sorted(set(x for x in files if x) | {'data/processed/oncokg.sqlite','data/processed/resolved.json'})
    return {p:hashlib.sha256((ROOT/p).read_bytes()).hexdigest() for p in files if (ROOT/p).is_file()}

def save(name, value):
    (OUT / name).write_text(json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding='utf-8')

def load_parent(name):
    dest = OUT / ('parent_' + name + '.py')
    dest.write_bytes(subprocess.check_output(['git', 'show', 'HEAD^:src/' + name + '.py'], cwd=ROOT))
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('m37_parent_' + name, dest)
    mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
    spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
    return mod

def compact(body):
    return {'state': next((k for k in ['center','refused','choose','alias_only','not_one_name','not_found','error'] if k in body), 'question'),
            'center': body.get('center', {}).get('id'),
            'choices': [c['id'] for c in body.get('choose', [])],
            'entities': body.get('entities'), 'evidence_count': len(body.get('evidence', []))}
